People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

work I’ve caught myself rocking in the hallway, copying a patient’s tapping, or standing there with my gloves half on like I’m stuck, when I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I feel heavy and empty from the time I wake up, and after shifts I’ll sit in my car crying before I can even drive home.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to sketch murals, cook for my friends, and play soccer on weekends, but now all of that feels pointless and I usually just ignore messages and stay in my apartment.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: I can’t really point to that as the reason for all this, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this difficulty keeping your thoughts together?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… longer than I want to make it sound. Maybe about a year and five months? It kind of started after my job changed from regular day shifts to rotating nights and a lot of overtime. I thought I was just adjusting badly, or tired, but it’s kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations, like seeing or hearing things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like seeing people who aren’t there or hearing voices talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I’m really tired and I might misread a sound or think someone called me, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like something it isn’t. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, empty, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up feeling really heavy, kind of empty. I try to push through and act normal, but after shifts sometimes I just sit in my car and cry for a while before I can even drive home. Is that… is that what you mean by depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overly worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like whether I’m doing things right or if people are upset with me, but I don’t know if it’s “overly” worried. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. What would count as too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things to the point where it interferes with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say it interferes a lot. I mean, I still get up and go to work and do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about whether I messed something up or said the wrong thing, and it can make it harder to focus, but… I’m not sure if that counts as interfering. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like extremely happy or energized. I get irritable sometimes, but I think that’s normal, like when I’m tired or stressed. My thoughts can feel busy, but I wouldn’t want to say “racing” if that means something more specific. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I thought I was just adjusting badly, or tired, but it’s kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations, like seeing or hearing things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. I mean… I’ve had better days where I’m in a decent mood or I get through work okay, but I wouldn’t say extremely happy or full of energy for a while. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually worry about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not usually. Maybe about a year and five months? It kind of started after my job changed from regular day shifts to rotating nights and a lot of overtime. I thought I was just adjusting badly, or tired, but it’s kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations, like seeing or hearing things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… hi. I guess I’m here because I’m having a hard time keeping my thoughts together when I talk. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but I’ll start telling someone about an overnight shift and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and people look like they’ve completely lost me. I know that’s not ideal. I just… I can’t keep my words in a straight line lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol or drugs recently?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I’m not doing drugs or anything like that. Alcohol… I don’t think it’s anything worth mentioning. I can’t really point to that as the reason for all this, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this difficulty keeping your thoughts together?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… longer than I want to make it sound. Maybe about a year and five months? It kind of started after my job changed from regular day shifts to rotating nights and a lot of overtime. I thought I was just adjusting badly, or tired, but it’s kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations, like seeing or hearing things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like seeing people who aren’t there or hearing voices talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I’m really tired and I might misread a sound or think someone called me, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like something it isn’t. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, empty, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up feeling really heavy, kind of empty. I try to push through and act normal, but after shifts sometimes I just sit in my car and cry for a while before I can even drive home. Is that… is that what you mean by depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overly worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like whether I’m doing things right or if people are upset with me, but I don’t know if it’s “overly” worried. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. What would count as too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things to the point where it interferes with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say it interferes a lot. I mean, I still get up and go to work and do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about whether I messed something up or said the wrong thing, and it can make it harder to focus, but… I’m not sure if that counts as interfering. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like extremely happy or energized. I get irritable sometimes, but I think that’s normal, like when I’m tired or stressed. My thoughts can feel busy, but I wouldn’t want to say “racing” if that means something more specific. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble relaxing when you're not working or actively doing something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I try to just sit and relax, but I end up pacing around my apartment or rubbing my hands together without really thinking about it, and then I notice they’re kind of sore.\n\nBut then other times it’s the opposite, I guess. People have told me I’m moving really slow, like… like I’m underwater or something. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or energized for a period of time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I’ve had better days where I’m in a decent mood or I get through work okay, but I wouldn’t say extremely happy or full of energy for a while. If that’s what you’re asking, I don’t think I’ve had that. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I mean, I worry sometimes, like anyone does, but I don’t know if it’s more days than not. I haven’t really kept track of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually worry about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not usually. Is that answer okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things like work, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I worry about that stuff sometimes, yeah, like work or money, but I don’t know if I’d say “often.” It doesn’t feel like it’s all the time or anything. I thought I was just adjusting badly, or tired, but it’s kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations, like seeing or hearing things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so.         ],
        "count": 3
      }
    ],
    "duration_verified": "symptoms present for over a year, since job change",
